to TheNP@googlegroups.com

Brothers,

I wanted to write and tell you two things:



First, you don't want to read any more Partnership Notes and I don't want to write them. The next time you
hear from me will probably be a few months from now.



Second I find myself full of thanksgiving. It would be easy to believe it to stem from the feeling that we won.
But that would be wrong not only because it isn't true of me but also because it isn't true of us. There was
plenty of "winning" in terms of votes and procedural motions and the mussels in roquefort broth and
McChouffe's at the Trappe Door, but that isn't what I mean. We didn't win; the kingdom won – and not
because Jesus is a member of the National Partnership (unless one of you invited him but I haven't heard
anything). The kingdom won because Bobby Griffith was passionate and because Jeff Kerr didn't fear the
microphone. The kingdom won because Scott Winchester came to the Overtures Committee without a suit on
and business proceeded nonetheless and because Dan Wykoff almost stayed awake the entire time we
debated the third substitute to a procedural motion regarding independent clause use in Appendix K.



The kingdom won because immediate bonds were formed between men in Greenville simply because they
shared the presence of the Spirit and union with Christ. They won because connecting your faces with your
names on the convention floor felt like meeting an old friend and because none of you felt weird that I kept
looking at your name tags. And because none of you made fun of me – to my face – about my West Wing-
esque brisk walk everywhere and because I did – I tell you I really did – at one point yell out "We're at
microphone FOUR!" outside the convention center like it was the most important thing that could be said.
Like the building might be on fire. Like the only escape was microphone FOUR.



The kingdom won because I read Fred Greko's nomination form – a man I sought to oppose by floor nominee
- and loved the story of his life. And it won because I had several chances to repent with men about whom I
said stupid things and I even took one or two of them. The kingdom won because at the end of the day I could
shake David Coffin's hand and thank him for helping me understand complicated things and because – God
help me – I do love Joey Pipa and that mustache because he's an old southern dog who still knows how to
wrangle an assembly, and because his sins are so, so familiar to mine.



The kingdom won too because one man stood in the courage of Jesus and rebuked us and him from the floor
of the General Assembly and said that we must suffer the little children; that a resurrection demands first the
grave for every one of us, even our sense of decorum, even our sense of prerogative. It demands that we
storm the gates full of fire sometimes and that we may have to dash ourselves against those gates in the
process. But Jesus is always with us and there is nothing lost between the last breath and the first.



There are no enemies for those who are in Christ, and though there is plenty of losing everywhere there are
no true defeats in the end. May you rest in your labors, brothers. Thank you.

JK

Brothers,

Thank you for your service to God's kingdom in the PCA - every day and last week at our
General Assembly.

Though Mike Sloan's resolution was not adopted, I will seek to introduce something similar for
my presbytery (North Texas) to adopt and send to all our member churches. I am going to send
drafts to child advocate attorneys and and make sure the language will be prophetic and specific
to my regional context. I urge some of you to try and do the same.

I would also like to see four or five presbyteries (or more) offer overtures regarding protecting
our children to the 42nd General Assembly. I think if we have more than one, even if the
subsequent ones read (answer in reference to Overture One) it will send a strong message to
those who are watching the PCA and to those who may be hesitant to "speak to legal issues" or
so-called "social justice" issues. It would be a nice homage to Mike's hard work if someone
could time it where Overture One next year is something like Mike's resolution.

It was an honor to sit with many of you at Microphone Four this year. I am grateful for all of you
and for this partnership.

Blessings,
Bobby

1. Additions (140 elders in 48 presbyteries)

2. Notes

I would propose these NP goals for the following 'season' (before GA 2014):

1. continue to dutifully serve on presbytery and assembly committees, fill vacant seats where possible

2. Support a healthy denominational dialogue - NP members speaking in GA seminars on topics beneficial to
the Church

3. Help to pass comprehensive guidance/advice on dealing with sexual abuse in the church via multiple
presbytery overtures

4. Resist presbytery-by-presbytery attempts to redefine/constrict orthodoxy in the PCA as they arise (similar
to opposition to intinction measures last year)

I would be glad to hear from you about these goals or others you would propose. Thanks for your help in
thinking this through.



TE Mike Sloan will be providing guidance to this group about the best way to move forward for those of us
interested in pursuing the matter of sexual abuse in the church. You should expect an update from Mike in the
next couple of days.



GA seminars: One way the NP can benefit our denomination is by starting helpful resourcing conversations at
GA through the seminar hours. Please suggest any seminars that you would love to hear at GA and we'll see
if anyone on this list is up for it. I would appreciate that week taking on more of a continuing education
opportunity. We will always have a fair amount of work to do at the assembly but wouldn't it be nice to also
benefit from the collective wisdom in our denomination? I'll start a conversation on the website regarding
possible topics and speakers. Here's what I've proposed to the folks in Atlanta so far (speakers for these topics
have been approached, though if any of you are interested in joining a panel discussion from one of the topics
below please let me know!):

1. Paper to pulpit: The sermon drafting process.

2. Presbytery Health: we have some guys in the NP that have pursued very helpful reforms in their
presbyteries. I would love to resource other pastors that long for a better life together for their presbyteries.

3. How to establish appropriate guidelines for dealing with sexual abuse in your church.

4. Staffing: Sharpening your Assistant and Associate Pastors for greater ministry impact.

5. The Pastor and Writer: cultivating your writing gifts to benefit both your local and the greater Church.

3. Calendar: for those of you new to our list or new to PCA polity, this is the typical lineup of those items
throughout the year requiring NP action,

Fall/Winter: Overtures from previous GA up for vote at the presbytery level. GA seminars are scheduled and
openings filled.

March: Nominating Committee meets in Atlanta to staff denominational permanent and special committees.

April: Review of Presbytery Records committee meets in Atlanta
May: Deadline for proposed BCO amendments. The NP starts discussions on all overtures set for GA

June: Houston in the Summer! Being a member of the NP presumes that you'll be with us at GA.

Dear Brothers,

Thank you for all of your encouragement during and since GA regarding the statement on child sexual abuse. I
wanted to update all of you on what will hopefully happen between now and next GA. I am organizing a
meeting with several PCA experts on child sexual abuse, including some survivors, which I hope will meet by
the end of the year. The goal of this meeting will be to produce a robust statement more in line with the
original statement I brought, not the weakened version that came out of the overtures committee. We will
also plan to produce a detailed rationale for the statement to give more education about the dynamics of
abuse and thus the need for a more robust statement. We will also address potential objections. In the
meeting we also plan to spend time planning strategically about how to encourage and educate churches in
this area so we can follow through with action not just words.

I am hoping there will be many men willing to take the statement coming out of this meeting to their
presbyteries who will send it up to the GA as an overture. This will accomplish two things. First, any
presbyteries who pass it will be sending the same consensus statement. It would be a nightmare if overtures
received several different statements and was charged with merging them somehow. Second, if the same
consensus statement is sent up by multiple presbyteries, it will send a message to overtures that our
denomination wants a strong statement, and that we should think carefully before we cut things out.

Two other quick notes:

First, as much as many of us want a statement from GA, remember you do not have to wait for the General
Assembly to pass a statement for your presbytery to act. I would encourage you all to work toward that end. I
would advise waiting until we have the consensus statement by the end of the year to see if you would like to
use it for your presbytery’s statement regardless of what GA in Houston decides.

Second, some of you have let me know you are willing to advocate for the consensus statement as an
overture to the GA from your presbytery. If you are willing, but have not communicated that to me, please let
me know soon so I can communicate well with you as this develops.

Please feel free to call me with any questions or concerns about this (678-524-5595). Thanks so much.

Your Servant,

Mike Sloan

2. News




TE Mike Sloan, who offered a personal resolution on the issue of child sexual abuse in the church at
Greenville's General Assembly, will be meeting with other denominational leaders in December to craft a
consensus statement for this year's Overtures Committee. If any of you are willing to speak for the consensus
statement by offering the same statement as an overture on the floor of your presbyteries, please contact
mike (mikea...@gmail.com). It will be easier to pass an overture in tact through the OC if several presbyteries
offer a version as similar as possible to the others. Here's a link to Mike's interview about child sexual abuse in
the church with ByFaith (http://byfaithonline.com/key-questions-about-child-sexual-abuse-in-the-church/).



The SJC is currently considering the three re-trial overtures (Calvary, Gulf Coast, MS Valley) regarding the TE
Leithart trial before PNW Presbytery. The Oct 17-18 meeting recessed and reconvenes Nov 4 for a likely
decision. The deeper question appears to be less about Leithart himself and more about whether BCO 34-1
applies in this particular case (34-1. Process against a minister shall be entered before the Presbytery of which
he is a member. However, if the Presbytery refuses to act in doctrinal cases or cases of public scandal and two
other Presbyteries request the General Assembly to assume original jurisdiction (to first receive and initially
hear and determine), the General Assembly shall do so.) that is, did PNW "refuse to act". Some in the
blogosphere have maintained that they (and many other, in their words) pastors and churches will depart the
PCA if the SJC refuses these appeals.



In light of the interest in the Leithart case and disagreement about the SJC's review process among some in
our denomination NP member Sean Lucas and his session crafted the language of overture 1
(http://theaquilareport.com/grace-presbytery-overtures-the-pca-ga-to-amend-bco-15-5-to-vote-on-sjc-
decisions/). While I'm not yet sure where I fall on the subject it's worth thinking through. I've created space
on the website here (http://thenationalpartnership.com/overture-1-voting-on-sjc-decisions/) and Sean has
answered a few questions about the overture's intent and effect. Please feel free to interact.



3. Calendar:

Fall/Winter: Overtures from previous GA up for vote at the presbytery level. GA seminars are scheduled and
openings filled.

March 22: Nominating Committee meets in Atlanta to staff denominational permanent and special
committees.
April 18: Overtures requiring CCB opinion

May 28-31: RPR (Review of Presbytery Records) meets in Atlanta.The NP starts discussions on all overtures set
for GA this month.

June: Houston in the Summer! Being a member of the NP presumes that you'll be in attendance at GA. We'll
try for an informal beer/pizza meeting during the week.

decision:http://byfaithonline.com/wp-content/uploads/2013/11/2013-SJC-Answer-to-Overtures-20-21-22-w-

regarding Overture 3. My own read is that we will need some sort of additional language regarding the SJC’s
review process, but that we haven’t yet seen that language. Check out the conversation here:
(http://thenationalpartnership.com/overture-1-voting-on-sjc-decisions/). Please feel free to interact.



3. Action Items

It’s time to think about committee assignments and committee of commissioners for the General Assembly.



Nominating Committee: This committee is tasked with filling the committees of our denomination with
qualified men. The committee meeting is March 22nd in Atlanta and your travel/lodging expenses are
reimbursed by the denomination. The work of this committee might really be the most efficient way to
contribute to the work of helping to lead our denomination. Those who sit on the NC will have to deliberate
together and work through the biographies of men submitted by their presbyteries. This is a critical
committee. Below are the presbyteries where we have Partnership members and there are either vacancies
or the term of the seated presbyter is expiring. Each presbytery rotates RE/TE every three year term. However
your presbytery may nominate based on availability (RE/TE). If you are in one of these presbyteries please

Review of Presbytery Records: Serving on RPR is a three-day commitment May 28-31 - and the PCA
pays the expenses. This committee reviews the minutes presbyteries submit to the General
Assembly to ensure the minutes of presbytery are in order – that is, there are no strange deviations,
exceptions recorded that strike at the vitals of our system, etc. What sounds perhaps mild-mannered
has become a battleground for theological axe-grinding. While there are many who seek to serve on
this committee for less litigious reasons, there are others who see RPR as an opportunity to move
the lines of orthodoxy in the PCA. It is always necessary to have wise and qualified men serving in
this committee to ensure that our denomination is accurately represented in these discussions. The
rules regarding the RE/TE alternating slots are similar to the NC. If there is not a qualified man of a
particular office willing to serve your presbytery may appoint one representative of the other office.

Covenant Theology?

One of my challenges is, in the class I have a few men who are really into this, but many men who

deal of knowledge and to the novice as well.

2. News
Westminster Presbytery has overtured the 42nd GA a second time to reaffirm a denominational stance
regarding homosexuality/same-sex marriage in stronger language than Overture 2 (also from Westminster
Presbytery). The page dealing with this Overture (Overture 2) is up on the Partnership website
(http://thenationalpartnership.com/overture-2-concerning-same-sex-marriage/). You can read about
overture 5 here (http://www.pcaac.org/wp-content/uploads/2014/01/Overture-5-Westminster-our-present-

proposal, I hope we can help him perfect it. (http://thenationalpartnership.com/the-order-of-widows-1-
timothy/)

There are currently 16 NP members serving on the Nominating Committee and 8 NP members serving on RPR

We have also begun a discussion on the website on Overture 6 (http://thenationalpartnership.com/overture-
6-child-protection-in-the-pca-georgia-foothills-presbytery/)

An appeal from Mike Sloan regarding Overture 6:
“Brothers,
I wanted to update you on where things stand. We had a productive meeting in
December with some PCA experts on child protection and came up with a robust
statement that I think will have a good chance to pass. My presbytery Georgia
Foothills passed it unanimously last Saturday (it is now officially Overture 6 from
Georgia Foothills on “Child Protection in the PCA”, available on the General Assembly
website). Now we are working to get other presbyteries to send a simple overture to
General Assembly urging that they pass the statement from Georgia Foothills. This
approach will allow us to get broad presbytery support without sending up ten
slightly different statements. What you can do in your presbytery is head up an effort
to pass this supporting overture: orchestrate bringing to the floor, speak for it, and
find others who will also speak for it. I have some supporting documentation and
arguments I would be happy to share with you that are helpful to understand why
this matters in the PCA. Also I will put up some rationale on the TNP discussion board
under this overture. I also have some simple straight-forward language you could use
for the overture:

'WHEREAS Georgia Foothills Presbytery has sent an overture to the 42nd General
Assembly calling on the General Assembly to speak to the issue of child sexual abuse
(Overture 6: “Child Protection in the PCA”); and

WHEREAS _____________________ Presbytery believes that child sexual abuse is an
issue to which the General Assembly should speak; and
WHEREAS _____________________ Presbytery endorses the substance of Overture
6 from Georgia Foothills Presbytery on child sexual abuse;

THEREFORE, be it RESOLVED that _____________________ Presbytery urges the
42nd General Assembly to answer in the affirmative Overture 6 from Georgia Foothills
Presbytery on child sexual abuse.'

I have already reached out to many of you about this and I am so encouraged by your
willingness to speak up on behalf of the little ones Jesus loves. Please pray for this.
Two presbyteries are considering a supporting overture today!"

regarding homosexuality/same-sex marriage in stronger language than Overture 2 (also from Westminster
Presbytery). The page dealing with this Overture (Overture 2) is up on the Partnership website
(http://thenationalpartnership.com/overture-2-concerning-same-sex-marriage/). You can read about
overture 5 here (http://www.pcaac.org/wp-content/uploads/2014/01/Overture-5-Westminster-our-present-
need.pdf) Feel free to interact.

(http://thenationalpartnership.com/new-blog/2014/2/4/nca6ppzyk8nc4zt7gh8acw3kxnacwq)

An appeal from Mike Sloan regarding Overture 6:
“Brothers,
I wanted to update you on where things stand. We had a productive meeting in December with some PCA
experts on child protection and came up with a robust statement that I think will have a good chance to pass.
My presbytery Georgia Foothills passed it unanimously last Saturday (it is now officially Overture 6 from
Georgia Foothills on “Child Protection in the PCA”, available on the General Assembly website). Now we are
working to get other presbyteries to send a simple overture to General Assembly urging that they pass the
statement from Georgia Foothills. This approach will allow us to get broad presbytery support without
sending up ten slightly different statements. What you can do in your presbytery is head up an effort to pass
this supporting overture: orchestrate bringing to the floor, speak for it, and find others who will also speak for
it. I have some supporting documentation and arguments I would be happy to share with you that are helpful
to understand why this matters in the PCA. Also I will put up some rationale on the TNP discussion board
under this overture. I also have some simple straight-forward language you could use for the overture:

'WHEREAS Georgia Foothills Presbytery has sent an overture to the 42nd General Assembly calling on the
General Assembly to speak to the issue of child sexual abuse (Overture 6: “Child Protection in the PCA”); and
WHEREAS _____________________ Presbytery believes that child sexual abuse is an issue to which the
General Assembly should speak; and

WHEREAS _____________________ Presbytery endorses the substance of Overture 6 from Georgia Foothills
Presbytery on child sexual abuse;

THEREFORE, be it RESOLVED that _____________________ Presbytery urges the 42 nd General Assembly to
answer in the affirmative Overture 6 from Georgia Foothills Presbytery on child sexual abuse.'

I have already reached out to many of you about this and I am so encouraged by your willingness to speak up
on behalf of the little ones Jesus loves. Please pray for this. Two presbyteries are considering a supporting
overture today!”


3. Action Items
Nominating Committee: This committee is tasked with staffing the committees of our denomination with
qualified men. The committee meeting is March 22nd in Atlanta and your travel/lodging expenses are
reimbursed by the denomination. The work of this committee might really be the most efficient way to
contribute to the work of helping to lead our denomination. Those who sit on the NC will have to deliberate
together and work through the biographies of men submitted by their presbyteries. This is a critical
committee. Below are the presbyteries where we have Partnership members and there are either vacancies
or the term of the seated presbyter is expiring. Each presbytery rotates RE/TE every three year term. However
your presbytery may nominate based on availability (RE/TE). If you are in one of these presbyteries please
consider joining in the work of this committee:

Chesapeake (TE)
Evangel (TE)
Gulf Stream (RE)
Metro Atlanta (RE)
North Texas (RE)
Northern Illinois (RE)
Philadelphia (TE)
Pittsburgh (TE)
Platte Valley (TE)
South Florida (TE)
Southwest (RE)
Suncoast (FL) (TE)

Review of Presbytery Records: Serving on RPR is a three-day commitment May 28-31 - and the PCA pays the
expenses. This committee reviews the minutes presbyteries submit to the General Assembly to ensure the
minutes of presbytery are in order – that is, there are no strange deviations, exceptions recorded that strike at
the vitals of our system, etc. What sounds perhaps mild-mannered has become a battleground for theological
axe-grinding. While there are many who seek to serve on this committee for less litigious reasons, there are
others who see RPR as an opportunity to move the lines of orthodoxy in the PCA. It is always necessary to
have wise and qualified men serving in this committee to ensure that our denomination is accurately
represented in these discussions. The rules regarding the RE/TE alternating slots are similar to the NC. If there
is not a qualified man of a particular office willing to serve your presbytery may appoint one representative of
the other office. Below is a list of NP presbyteries where there is a vacancy.

Central Indiana (TE)
Chesapeake (RE)
Evangel (RE)
Gulf Stream (TE)
Illiana (TE)
Iowa (TE)
North Texas (TE)
Northern California (TE)
Northern Illinois (TE)
Philadelphia (RE)
Pittsburgh (RE)
Platte Valley (RE)
Potomac (RE)
South Florida (RE)
Southwest (TE)
Suncoast (FL) (RE)

try for an informal beer/pizza meeting during the week.

An appeal from Mike Sloan regarding Overture 6:
“Brothers,
I wanted to update you on where things stand. We had a productive meeting in December with some PCA
experts on child protection and came up with a robust statement that I think will have a good chance to pass.
My presbytery Georgia Foothills passed it unanimously last Saturday (it is now officially Overture 6 from
Georgia Foothills on “Child Protection in the PCA”, available on the General Assembly website). Now we are
working to get other presbyteries to send a simple overture to General Assembly urging that they pass the
statement from Georgia Foothills. This approach will allow us to get broad presbytery support without
sending up ten slightly different statements. What you can do in your presbytery is head up an effort to pass
this supporting overture: orchestrate bringing to the floor, speak for it, and find others who will also speak for
it. I have some supporting documentation and arguments I would be happy to share with you that are helpful
to understand why this matters in the PCA. Also I will put up some rationale on the TNP discussion board
under this overture. I also have some simple straight-forward language you could use for the overture:
'WHEREAS Georgia Foothills Presbytery has sent an overture to the 42nd General Assembly
calling on the General Assembly to speak to the issue of child sexual abuse (Overture 6: “Child
Protection in the PCA”); and
WHEREAS _____________________ Presbytery believes that child sexual abuse is an issue to
which the General Assembly should speak; and
WHEREAS _____________________ Presbytery endorses the substance of Overture 6 from
Georgia Foothills Presbytery on child sexual abuse;
THEREFORE, be it RESOLVED that _____________________ Presbytery urges the 42 nd General
Assembly to answer in the affirmative Overture 6 from Georgia Foothills Presbytery on child
sexual abuse.’

-Overture 3 from Grace Presbytery, which seeks to allow for greater review of SJC decisions, has been affirmed by
overtures from five other presbyteries. This is going to be the primary issue debated in Houston. Unfortunately
these proposed revisions appear to many to plunge us back into Assembly bickering on judicial cases, and will
encourage greater politicizing of decisions in the blogosphere and elsewhere.

-Men are needed for the Overtures Committee. I have 7 so far noted below. Please let us know if you have jumped
on the OC.

-Please let me know what committees you’ll be serving so I can update our website; the current list is below – let
me know where there are corrections to be made

-There is still time for your presbytery to pass an affirmation of Overture 6 regarding sexual abuse in the church.
The form for doing so is included in this email




The following is a message from Mike Khandjian, NP member and organizer of The Fellowship Dinner. His group
was created completely independent of ours but with a high degree of overlap. Where his group is invested in
fellowship and visioning we are more engaged in logistics. Both groups are necessary and helpful! He wanted to
extend an invitation to those of you on this list:

"I can't begin to tell you how cool this evening will be. Kevin Twit will lead us in some song, but he will be
accompanied by the worship band from Redeemer Sugarland, the church Bradley Wright pastors in the Houston
area. They too will lead in some song and the music will flow as we gather and eat - it will b

e a bash. As to the dinner, DON'T ASSUME YOU ARE ON THE GUEST LIST!!! I make no assumptions as to who is
coming, unless contacted. As it stands, we are over 1/3 full for the evening, which is way ahead of where we were
last year at this time. The word is out and it has to be a first-come, first-seated dinner. What is this Dinner? - I
guess the best way to put it is that it is an evening - a couple hours - where we rehearse the Gospel to ourselves
and celebrate who we were always intended to be as a denomination, and are. Ultimately, though politics matter
in the whole matter of change, it will be a gathering community of people who are determined to protect the
sweeter things of the Faith - in how we present ourselves, how we love one another and how we love the world
God created to be good, that makes every aspect of our involvement worthwhile. "

The following is a message from Mike Khandjian, NP member and organizer of The Fellowship Dinner. His group
was created completely independent of ours but with a high degree of overlap. Where his group is invested in
fellowship and visioning we are more engaged in logistics. Both groups are necessary and helpful! He wanted to
extend an invitation to those of you on this list:
"I can't begin to tell you how cool this evening will be. Kevin Twit will lead us in some song, but he will be
accompanied by the worship band from Redeemer Sugarland, the church Bradley Wright pastors in the Houston
area. They too will lead in some song and the music will flow as we gather and eat - it will be a bash. As to the
dinner, DON'T ASSUME YOU ARE ON THE GUEST LIST!!! I make no assumptions as to who is coming, unless
contacted. As it stands, we are over 1/3 full for the evening, which is way ahead of where we were last year at this
time. The word is out and it has to be a first-come, first-seated dinner. What is this Dinner? - I guess the best way
to put it is that it is an evening - a couple hours - where we rehearse the Gospel to ourselves and celebrate who we
were always intended to be as a denomination, and are. Ultimately, though politics matter in the whole matter of
change, it will be a gathering community of people who are determined to protect the sweeter things of the Faith -
in how we present ourselves, how we love one another and how we love the world God created to be good, that
makes every aspect of our involvement worthwhile. “ You can contact Mike about the Fellowship Dinner
at mi...@chapelgate.org

Review of Presbytery Records: Serving on RPR is a three-day commitment May 28-30 - and the PCA pays the
expenses. This committee reviews the minutes presbyteries submit to the General Assembly to ensure the minutes
of presbytery are in order – that is, there are no strange deviations, exceptions recorded that strike at the vitals of
our system, etc. What sounds perhaps mild-mannered has become a battleground for theological axe-grinding.
While there are many who seek to serve on this committee for less litigious reasons, there are others who see RPR
as an opportunity to move the lines of orthodoxy in the PCA. It is always necessary to have wise and qualified men
serving in this committee to ensure that our denomination is accurately represented in these discussions. The rules
regarding the RE/TE alternating slots are similar to the NC. If there is not a qualified man of a particular office
willing to serve your presbytery may appoint one representative of the other office. Below is a list of NP
presbyteries where there is a vacancy.

Central Indiana (TE)
Chesapeake (RE)
Evangel (RE)
Gulf Stream (TE)
Illiana (TE)

Northern California (TE)
Northern Illinois (TE)
Philadelphia (RE)
Pittsburgh (RE)
Platte Valley (RE)
Potomac (RE)
South Florida (RE)
Southwest (TE)
Suncoast (FL) (RE)

Overture from Pittsburgh Presbytery
1 view

jon

May 14, 2014, 9:34:05 AM

to thenp@googlegroups.com
Brothers,



My Session presented an Overture to Presbytery in January that received a unanimous vote. It was
delivered to the Clerk prior to the deadline, but somehow was never forwarded to the CCB. We’ve
been told that it has been forwarded to the CCB, and will be added as an Assembly addition
(pending their approval). While it’s not as important of an overture as some that we’ll be considering,
I’d like to get it in front of the Partnership for you to be aware of, and interact with any questions you
might have regarding it.



The Overture asks that BCO 18-7 be amended regarding removal of candidates and interns. We are
proposing that the reasons (and any action taken by a Session or Presbytery) for removal be
reported in the minutes of Presbytery. It seems to us that we are not caring well for our candidates
and interns by not having this stipulation. We’ve often noticed that men are removed ‘just because,’
and then later find out that there was some contention or lack of care by a pastor or Session, but by
that time there’s no way for the Presbytery to intervene. We’ve also noticed, (and unfortunately been
involved in a situation before my time at Covenant Community) where questionable actions by an
intern were cause for his removal, but not reported to Presbytery, only for those issues to show
themselves later and in ‘greater’ sin. Had the Presbytery known of these issues, perhaps greater
care would have been given to help these men confront their sin, and find freedom in Christ.



Anyway, I wanted to pass this along to you men so that you may prayerfully consider it for this
summer’s GA. Also, if you have any questions or concerns, please feel free to pass them along.



Blessings,



Jon Price
Pastor
Covenant Community Church



OVERTURE FROM PITTSBURGH PRESBYTERY "Amend BCO 18-7 Regarding Removal of Candidates and
Interns" Whereas every intern is required to be a candidate for ministry (BCO 19-8), and; Whereas with
respect to the candidate "his preparatory training for the ministry he is under the oversight of the
Presbytery. It shall be the duty of the Presbytery to show a kindly and sympathetic interest in him" [BCO
18-4, emphasis added]; and Whereas "The intern should be closely supervised by the Presbytery
throughout this trial period." [BCO 19-7, emphasis added]; and Whereas "For the development of his
Christian character, for the service he can render, and for his more effective training, the candidate,
when entering on his theological studies, should be authorized and encouraged by the Presbytery to
conduct public worship, to expound the Scriptures to the people, and to engage in other forms of
Christian work. These forms of service should be rendered under the direction of Presbytery" [SCO 18-5];
and Whereas this relationship between the candidate and Presbytery does not change when they
become an intern, "Presbyteries should require interns to devote themselves diligently to the trial of
their gifts; and no one should be ordained to the work of the ministry of the Word until he has
demonstrated the ability both to edify and to rule in the Church." (BCO 19-12]; and Whereas the
candidate or intern can have his status removed. "A candidate shall, at his request, be allowed to
withdraw from the care of the Presbytery. The Presbytery may also, for sufficient reasons, remove the
name of the candidate from its roll of candidates; but in such a case it shall report its actions and the
reasons therefor to the candidate and to the Session of his church." [BCO 18-7]; and Whereas the
Presbytery is required to give sufficient reasons for the candidate's removal to the candidate and to the
Session of his church [BCO 18-7]; and Whereas Sessions will, at their discretion, request the removal of
candidates from the care of the Presbytery; and Whereas the Session of his church is not required to give
sufficient reasons or make a report to Presbytery of its actions; and Whereas there is no requirement for
the candidate to give sufficient reasons to the Presbytery for his withdraw from the care of the
Presbytery; and Whereas we are doing a disservice to these men under our care by allowing them, or
their Sessions, to request to be withdrawn from the care of the Presbytery without reporting sufficient
reasons and actions, thereby not giving the Presbytery the opportunity to "show a kindly and
sympathetic interest in him" [BCO 18-4] before the relationship is severed. Therefore be it resolved that
the 42nd General Assembly of the Presbyterian Church in America amend BCO 18-7 by adding "or at the
request of his Session,... But in such a case sufficient reasons [and any actions taken] must be reported
to the Presbytery." Also, "In all cases of a removal or withdrawal of a candidate, the sufficient reason for
the action shall be recorded in the minutes of Presbytery." The new 18-7 would thus read [new wording
underlined]: "The Presbytery may, upon appHcation of the candidate, give a certificate of dismission to
another Presbytery. The candidate may be allowed to retain membership in his home church upon the
request of his Session and the approval of both Presbyteries involved. A candidate shall, at his request or
at the request of his Session, be allowed to withdraw from the care of the Presbytery, by reporting
sufficient reasons to the Presbytery. But in such a case sufficient reasons (and any actions taken] must be
reported to the Presbytery. The Presbytery may also, for sufficient reasons, remove the name of the
candidate from its roll of candidates; but in such a case it shall report its actions and the reasons
therefore to the candidate and to the Session of his church. In all cases of a removal or withdrawal of a
candidate, the sufficient reason for the action shall be recorded in the minutes of Presbytery." Adopted
by Pittsburgh Presbytery at its stated meeting, January 25, 2014. Stated Clerk

Brothers,

In an effort to ensure that everyone participating in the National Partnership feels no undue pressure I want
to extend the offer to de-list from us if you would like; if your conscience pushes you toward non-
participation you should feel free to be removed. I have been very thankful for all of your participation in the
Partnership – it has been encouraging for me and helpful for my growth as a presbyter (and a pastor!). As we
head into General Assembly you’ll receive quite a few updates and exhortations to be present for votes, etc. It
would be natural to hang on to membership in the NP because all of us want to “know what people are up
to.” But the NP is a working group and we would hope/expect that you’ll participate in the process rather
than sit on the sidelines. You should feel no frustration from me or others if you decide it isn’t for you. Just let
me know and I’ll try to change our roster as quickly as possible. Again, whatever your decision, I’m grateful for
your participation so far!



For the rest of you: let’s get ready for Houston.

JK

General Assembly Note #1

James Kessler

to TheNP@googlegroups.com

[The following communication is confidential and may not be shared without the permission of the sender]

Partnership Website: http://thenationalpartnership.com (password: ‘rev223’)



Brothers,

This evening the Assembly begins with the first worship service and the election of a moderator. I wanted to
give you an overview of this week’s work. I will send it in multiple emails for ease of navigation:



I. 42nd Assembly Introduction – primary issues (now)

II. Study Committee on Insider Movements (later tonight)

III. The overtures committee review (Wednesday morning)

IV. Nominations to permanent committee (wednesday evening)



As I have said before, I will do my best to provide resources for your preparation – including my own
perspectives – but I want to emphasize once again that participation in the National Partnership does not
imply your conformity to the opinions expressed. What I’m going for is unity – in truth and charity. Certainly
the opinions expressed here would be far less helpful had I not received from you all much feedback and
help. Thank you!



Overview

The primary issues

The SJC’s current system allows for the commission to process a case without waiting for the Assembly to
either uphold or strike down their decisions. This year there are a handful of motions seeking to allow for
decisions of the SJC to be reviewed but the Assembly before they are finalized. The Overtures committee
recommended that all the SJC motions be answered in the negative. While there was much debate the
decision was not narrow – they failed by a 4-1 margin in committee. Next they will come to the floor where
the Assembly can debate the motion to answer the SJC overtures in the negative but they cannot offer
amendments. This means either the assembly will affirm the motion to answer the overtures in the negative
(I.e. ‘no to SJC changes’) or they will deny the motion to answer in the negative, committing the matter to the
next Overtures committee at the general assembly in 2015.



Overtures 2 and 5 sought to make a denominational statement in regards to the issue of homosexuality and
the sanctity of marriage. The makers of the overture want the denomination to go on record against the
encroachment of religious liberty by groups advocating for homosexual rights (in view: christians denying
their services to homosexual couples on religious grounds then being prosecuted) and to reaffirm the biblical
doctrine of marriage over and against the current cultural trend. The debate in the Overtures committee was
very passionate and more divided than any other debate. On one side were those who thought the motions
unhelpful pastorally in contexts where churches are seeking to engage the culture more delicately. On the
other side were those who feel anxiety about the increasing harassment Christians receive in trying to live out
Biblical commitments. Both sides want the Church of Jesus Christ to speak pastorally and truthfully to the
issue but disagree as to whether an overture to the general assembly resulting in a public statement is the
best forum. Ultimately after much debate and reconsidering the question the overtures were answered in the
negative – a good result in my view. There will be a minority report to answer in the affirmative with some
amendments. This means that on the floor of the assembly the minority report can be substituted for the
majority report by a majority of the commissioners voting. I expect this debate to be fevered on the floor.



The Study Committee on Insider Movements is docketed for discussion tomorrow afternoon. The question
before the Assembly will be this: 1. Accept only the Majority report (deny minority report) 2. Accept both the
Majority and Minority report (treating them as companion work of the study committee). It is my hope that
the Minority report will be accepted. If you are looking to understand more about the minority report here
are some links I found to be helpful:

I want to recommend two seminars to you. Unfortunately they are occurring simultaneously, Wednesday
morning - 8am. They are both strategically important – the relationships on multi staff churches have great
potential to multiply ministry and empower movements; the transformation of presbytery culture is a crucial
step toward greater impact in church planting, mercy ministry and community transformation. The men
leading these seminars are great teachers. I hope you’ll make time for one of them:

process of moving presbyter- ies toward being an inward-facing “court of care” for pastors and an outward-

Indiana Presbyteries.



A point of personal privilege

A good friend reminded me of a helpful truth a couple of weeks ago: we must constantly fight against the
tendency to reduce the personhood of those with whom we disagree to a proposition easily dismissed. More
simply: your opponents are more than the propositions they affirm. We have to fight to hear each other in
these heated debates. I tell you I am not good at this. I am impatient and weary of anyone’s voice but my own
after a few minutes. I pray that you and I will fight the good fight to love Jesus well in these conversations.
May we be alternately frustrated, relieved, pensive, winsome, annoyed even, but never disgusted with one
another. When we throw away our voting cards on Friday; when we beat our swords back into plowshares we
will continue to drink from the same fountain and we will continue to plead the same blood. A pleading much
louder and sharper than our disagreements. I know you know all of this but it does my heart good to say
them out loud again. I want to excel at being a churchman this week. I believe that means I must excel as a
worshiper too. Thank you as always for joining in the hard work of loving the brethren well and leading this
small part of the Kingdom. Many many blessings to you.



Warmly,

JK

                              PCA Study Committee for Insider Movements
                                         Minority Report 2014 FAQs


Prelude


As an aid to Commissioners to the 2014 General Assembly of the PCA regarding the Study
Committee on Insider Movements, the authors of the 2014 Minority Report entitled, “Realities
on the Ground, An Additional Perspective,” present the following FAQs.


Question: Why did you submit a Minority Report to the 2014 General Assembly?

Answer: While the Committee Report (CR) provides vital theological foundations and helpful
boundaries for doing mission (through the Affirmations and Denials) which we support, we
believe it's important for those doing mission among Muslims to receive information concerning
certain on-the-ground realities, along with some associated biblical considerations. We hope
and pray that the additional perspective in this 37 page report will provide practical help to PCA
churches, assisting them in their broad sowing of the message of God’s saving power through
his Son and the building of his church throughout Muslim nations, “…that your ways may be
known on earth, your salvation among all nations” (Psalm 67:2, NIV).

Question: What is the relationship between MR 2014 and the Minority Report submitted
during the General Assembly of 2013?

Answer: MR 2014 has new authorship and thus we consider it as a total replacement for
Minority Report 2013.

Question: What is the significance of MR 2014’s recommendations to the 2014 General
Assembly?

Answer: Our recommendations mirror those of the CR with the exception that we separately
recommend that the 2014 General Assembly, "make available and recommend for study" both
the CR and MR 2014 to its presbyteries, churches and missions committees. Treating the papers
separately allows commissioners to discuss and vote for each paper on its own merit. A General
Assembly action to “make available and recommend for study” does not imply agreement with
every word or idea expressed in a paper. We are not requesting, “adoption” of these reports,
but that the Assembly send the papers to presbyteries, churches and missions committees for
study.


Question: Tell us some of your convictions on matters related to Insider Movement missiology.


   1 We hold to the Scriptures as our only authoritative guide to engaging in mission.
   2 We believe that both Scripture and our confession encourage believers to apply
      scriptural principles to the realities of everyday life, wherever they live.
   3 We believe that Christ ordinarily intends that his people will follow him in the context of
      their family, birth community, and vocation.
   4 In recognition of the comprehensive claims of Christ on the lives of his people we hold
      that identity in Christ is wholly controlling in the life of the believer; we do not advocate
      or support voluntary, indefinite retention of Islamic religious identity by MBBs.
   5 We hold that disciplers of MBBs should not encourage a disciple to remain within Islamic
      religious institutions.
   6 We hold that every believer is a member of the church of Jesus Christ, and we believe in
      the central importance of every believer to be part of a local expression of church.
   7 We hold by faith that Muslims are people made in the image of God, and that through
      Christ alone, people from Muslim majority countries will be among those represented
      before the throne of God (Rev. 7:9-10).


   (Note: In Minority Report 2014, references are provided for the above points to specific Affirmations
   and Denials from the Majority Report.)



Question: What is the outline of the Minority Report?


Answer: After providing introductory context to this report, we describe five realities faced by
believers living in Muslim societies and treat four considerations that undergird and inform our
approach to mission in the midst of these realities. A list of questions for use by missions
committees with their missions partners is also included. An outline of our major topics follows.


Part One: Realities on the Ground Facing Muslim Background Believers (MBBs)
   8 Reality #1: It is Important that MBBs Live Biblically within Muslim Societies.
   9 Reality #2: MBBs Can Live Biblically within Muslim Societies.
   10 Reality #3: National Churches within Muslim Societies Do Not Always Accept MBBs.
   11 Reality #4: Living within Muslim Societies Requires MBBs to be Vigilant to Avoid
       Syncretism.
   12 Reality #5: Growing in Christ within Muslim Societies Holds Significant Challenges for
       MBBs.

Part Two: Biblical Considerations for Facing Realities on the Ground.
   13 Consideration A: Every Culture has “Good” and Evil Aspects.
   14 Consideration B: We Must Not Add Requirements to the Gospel: Principles from the
       Jerusalem Council of Acts 15.
   15 Consideration C: We are Called to Live in the World But Not of the World:
       1 Corinthians 7:17-24 and its Context.
   16 Consideration D: We Must Not Participate with Demons in False Worship: A Warning
       from 1 Corinthians 10:19-20.

Brothers,
As you prepare to vote on the Overtures before us here is a preview with recommendations – as always these
are suggestions that you are free to accept or not! Hopefully this information will aid you.

First the rules:
-No one on the Overtures committee can enter into debate on the floor – they have already spoken through
committee
-Each overture typically comes with a recommendation to affirm or answer in the negative.
If the recommendation is to answer in the affirmative:
The floor can affirm the committee and the overture is passed
The floor can answer the committee in the negative and the overture is not passed
The floor can move to recommit the overture to either the current Overtures committee or next year’s
If the recommendation is to answer in the negative:
The floor can affirm the committee and the overture is not passed
The floor can answer the committee in the negative and the overture is not passed, but is automatically
recommitted to the overtures committee
-No one may offer an amendment to the work of the overtures committee. All perfecting of language must be
done by the OC.

    1. All SJC motions were answered in the negative in reference to Overture 13 (that means all were dealt
       with together). The vote was by a 4-1 margin. There will be considerable debate but not much that
       can be done. Either these motions will be completely defeated by affirming the committee or they
       will be recommitted probably to next year’s overtures committee for further work. I am
       recommending against recommitting these overtures as none of them can improve our current
       process and all would lead to difficulties of all sorts. There are many fine summaries out there but I
       am including an attachment to something I wrote regarding this – I was helped along by many others
       in my opinion.
    2. Overture 6: the child protection overture was answered in the affirmative (unanimously) and should
       be affirmed by all of us. Here are some additional notes from Mike Sloan who coauthored the
       overture:
    3.
"Brothers, the child protection overture received unanimous affirmation in the
Overtures Committee (there was 1 abstention). There were a few edits, but in my
opinion these were all friendly, and in no way water down what was intended. I do
not anticipate much opposition on the floor, but there were some concerning
comments by David Coffin in his overture chart. You never know what will happen on
the floor. I cannot speak to it because as a member of the Overtures Committee I am
not to allow to enter into the debate on overtures when they come to the floor. If
needed I hope that you will be ready to stand up for the precious little ones that
Jesus welcomes with open arms as many of you already have in your presbyteries.
Thank you for all of your encouragement to me this year. I ask your indulgence for
one final, personal word, my dear brothers. I am grateful for the rising awareness
surrounding child protection. I am also aware this overture consists only of words.
Words matter, but without action, they are empty. My prayer is that we will speak
clearly on Thursday, but also take these words as a call to action in our churches.




Read quickly!

SJC argument:



Men,

I hope you’ll permit me to have the last word on our SJC discussion before we move on. I’ve asked for
and received quite a bit of advice concerning the seven overtures related to the SJC and I’ll put them
here for the benefit of the group. Attached you’ll find one document prepared by a very gifted brother in
our denomination. I was thankful to receive it and also offer it to you as help. Here are my own thoughts
after hearing from you and many others:



Proposed changes create unacceptable delays in process:

I thought Ray Cannata had some helpful things to say here, but I also asked advice from multiple
members of the SJC who are also a part of the NP and they are universally disturbed by the idea of delay
in a case that may have already taken a few years to process through the courts. A case decided in the
SJC October meeting, for instance, could not be finalized for 8 more months! In some cases it may not be
of too much consequence but in cases where a minister’s good name (or employment!) is on the line,
this is a grievous wait for justice. In the case of a Presbytery commission (whose decisions must be
finalized by the entire body) the delay would likely be no more than a month or so, from stated meeting
to stated meeting.



Proposed changes expose parties to an uninformed and politicized jury

Every person I’ve spoken to who has served on the SJC has been humbled by the massive amounts of
reading and deliberation necessary to dispose of a single case before the court. It is foolish to think that
even the most conscientious commissioner will have the opportunity to read the record of any but the
most notorious cases before them. And this is part of the problem. If only the most notorious cases
receive this degree of review and discussion among commissioners, we have entered a world where
justice is not equal from case to case, because one court is uninterested in 95% of the plaintiff cases. The
issue is this: the Assembly as a whole cannot justly function as a sometimes-interested court of review. It
must function uniformly despite the case presented before it, or not at all.



Further to this point, notorious cases create a climate where half-truths and uninformed theories breed.
In our secular court system the process of sequestration protects defendants and plaintiffs from
politicized juries. In our current SJC system the final word on a case proceeds from sequestered
discovery and deliberation. The records of the case are made available to the commission and decisions
rendered before they ever hit the blogosphere – the record of the case is not as easily compromised by
widespread commentary. This is the proper way to decide a judicial case, extending mercy to the parties
by a concern to limit the record of the case accordingly. David Coffin’s ByFaith rejoinder on these
overtures covers the oaths SJC members must take before deliberation and voting – it may be helpful to
review these.



Proposed changes presume a mistrust of commission work

Much of the discussion over these changes revolves around the following fear: that a commission
appointed by the General Assembly (I.e. To function as the assembly) will err so grievously that a slide
into liberalism and disunion will follow, with no recourse. The watchword is “lack of accountability”. Here
are my problems with this assertion:

1. The Assembly has established the SJC with safeguards: a minority report consisting of at least 1/3 of
the commission may be presented to the Assembly for vote and the Nominating Committee yearly elects
6 members of the court submitted through the Presbyteries – allowing the assembly to turn over 1/4 of
the court’s membership every year. A quick comparison to the Federal court system (lifetime
appointments) provide a stark contrast.

2. The General Assembly is itself a commission! Commissions are Presbyterian! 700-1000 commissioners
yearly vote on matters significant to thousands more who are not present. One could say that cases
decided by the Assembly, itself a small commission in comparison to the whole of Teaching Elders and
eligible Ruling Elders would be functioning in the same capacity as our current commission but without
the same skill and knowledge of the case, and after great delay.

3. If a Commissioner cannot trust the decision of the Presbytery, nor the decision of the commission of
his own Assembly reviewing the Presbytery, nor the process for reforming the erring commission (voting
new members in), the commissioner’s greater problem may be his inability to fulfill his fourth ordination
vow (subjection to the brethren) because he does not trust his fellow Elders to dispose of any business in
which he is not directly empowered.



ADDITIONAL NOTES:

This is an emotional debate. I think it is advantageous, if you wish to oppose these overtures, to engage
that emotion. Commissions are an expression of connectionalism: we trust each other to labor in the
same field, out of view, for our own good. Or we don’t.



While it is tempting to make this conversation about one case, we must remember that this isn’t about
one case. For Sean Lucas this is about the case of the future and the rogue presbytery of the future and
the court whose hands are tied. The question every commissioner must ask himself is this: are we willing
to lose charity and care for the parties in these cases because we are fearful of an imagined possible
future? It really is that simple of a choice: the seven overtures seeking some improvement or even a
middle ground seem to fail, in our opinion, to improve anything. This is evidence that we must choose
one of two futures for our life together at the Assembly: will it be a dull meeting of trusting though
diligent commissioners or will it be a shootout with the interested parties fighting every notorious case
on the floor and uninformed commissioners seeking cover? Our preliminary principles favor the former.
The latter really is a different denomination, not the PCA.



Thanks for allowing me to speak on this. I want to be careful not to coerce anyone; please forgive me if I
am being too forward. We’ll start discussion on other amendments Thursday.

JK

There is a minority report regarding Overture 43 (by which overtures 2 and 5 are answered in the negative).
The floor has the option of, after hearing the minority opinion, to substitute the minority report for the
majority report. In this case it is to answer with a statement for prayer and encouragement to those pastors
engaging in Marriage and sanctity of human life issues. While I personally do not believe that a statement like
this will actually equip pastors and open new avenues of Kingdom. Here is an argument from Kevin Twit
regarding Overture 43




Thoughts on Minority Report for Overture 43

I am opposed to this softened version of Overture 43 and since I am unable to speak
during overtures, I am willing to share my thoughts for those who are interested.

Even thought the minority report softens (and certainly improves) the resolves, it
does not eliminate, nor speak to, the original overture in the decisive way I believe
we should. I don’t think overtures that ask the PCA to pick and choose certain issues
to highlight through public proclamation are helpful approaches and believe we
should send a strong message that such overtures will be rejected. In this case,
overtures 2 and 5 were overwhelmingly rejected, but many felt that we should say
something and overture 43 was seen as a good compromise. But I want to speak
clearly that overtures such as these are unwise and we need to stop sending them.

Even though the resolve is softened in the minority report approach, the where as
section is still there. I know that we don’t adopt the where as section, but who
outside of General Assembly commissioners really understands that. If we send this
out publically, people will assume we agree with the where as section. The first
problem with the where as section is that is references the US Constitution – but the
PCA does not just contain people in the United States and so it fails to recognize that
fact. How are our pastors and churches in Canada to explain this overture?

Furthermore, overtures which seek to have the whole church speak to an issue,
end up making it more difficult for our churches to contextualize the Bible’s
teaching in their own contexts. Undoubtedly in the PCA there are pastors working in
church contexts where they could lose credibility for not speaking a word of solidarity
for those suffering for their stand on gay marriage and sanctity of life. But there are
also pastors working in contexts where speaking out on these issues must be dealt
with very skillfully, because most of the people in their community assume that all
the church cares about is condemning things like gay marriage. Broad statements like
this are a bad idea – we need to allow pastors and churches freedom to know how to
speak, and when to speak, in their own context. What seems like a bold stand in one
area of the country may come across as an unnecessary offense in another. I am not
saying that we must weaken our stance on sanctity or life or marriage. We are
confessional Presbyterians and thus it is clear where we stand on these issues. But
overtures like this demand we consider the wisdom of how and when we speak to
these issues publically – and I would argue such wisdom is best worked out and
applied at a local level.

We must be very careful when we speak as a church about issues, particularly
those that are seen as connected to the culture war in our day. Even the softened
version of the resolves still carries the impression that we as a church are cherry
picking certain issues to care about and remaining silent on other issues.
Furthermore, as the grounds for answering this original overture in the negative say,
“It is vital that the world hear the gospel from us clearly, yet overtures like this can
add weight to the charge that we are more interested in condemning the culture
than we are in reaching the culture with the love of Christ.”

The minority report alters this overture in such a way that it really seeks to propose
a different overture – and thus we end up dealing with an overture not from a
Presbytery but from a minority of the overtures committee. If those in the minority
want an overture such as the minority report proposes, let them work on in with
input from their presbytery (typically the way we gain the wisdom of the broader
church on a proposed overture) and let them bring it in more typical way. I do not
believe we need to affirm the practice of rewriting an overture in such a radical way
by a minority of the overtures committee.
While I appreciate the intent of those who asked us to reconsider overture 43 in
the overtures committee in order to avoid having the General Assembly deal with a
minority report, this strategy was never going to work. If we had adopted the
minority report as our response to overture 43 then we would have had the original
answer to overture 43, with the grounds, offered as a minority report. And it is worth
mentioning that this minority report was already offered up to the overtures
committee as a substitute motion and it was rejected.

to TheNP@googlegroups.com

Deep gratitude to all of you for your hard work at the General Assembly this week. For two straight
assemblies I saw the majority of the PCA well-represented in committees, on the floor of the assembly, at
gatherings late at night and into the early hours of the morning. As one who felt like an alien at the GA as
recently as three years ago I am so thankful. You were there and you were ready to speak, ready to vote.
Great men were elected to committee work and for the most part the actions of the assembly were beneficial
for the growth of the Kingdom. I could not have imagined it in Louisville.
During one of the seminars Ray Cortese spoke about his presbytery being a place he would not hesitate to
bring unbelievers (by the way you should get the tape from that seminar with Bruce O’Neil and Jason Dorsey;
incredibly helpful). I felt there were many times this week when I would have proudly brought a non-Christian
around with me; when we reflected a PCA that can actually speak in a culture where you have to earn your
voice with grace, truth and love.



Thank you Joel St. Clair who kept me from making too many administrative mistakes and handled a huge
burden for the Nominating Committee. Thanks to David Richter who sat in on the entire OC meeting even
though he wasn’t a member and helped me understand the issues we would be facing from the floor. Thanks
to Bryan Chapell who did not wait the extra second to hear calls for ‘division’. Thanks to Mike Sloan whose
Child Protection Overture gave me one concrete thing I could bring to my church as worth the price of the
Assembly. Thanks to sweaty Mike Khandjian (blame Houston) who opened my eyes to the fact that with 300
in attendance at Wednesday night’s dinner I am not nearly as hip as I might have thought – this assembly is
full of brilliant and holy men who can hold a pint or five. Thank you all for reminding me by your example that
though I was not always at my best this week, Jesus was.



You will not hear from me for a few months, so enjoy that! Let’s make it three-in-a-row in Chattanooga.

Warmly,

JK

Brothers,
We’re back at it. We need to start thinking about nominations to permanent
committees, the SJC, etc. What I mean by that is participation in the work of the PCA
means that we can suggest men at the presbytery level that will be healthy additions
to places of significant influence in our church. For those of you who are unaware of
the process:
    Men are nominated to the permanent committees at the Presbytery level.
    Those nominations are debated and confirmed at the Nominating Committee meeting in March
    Those nominees approved by the Nominating committee come to the floor of the General Assembly
         for a vote
     Floor nominations are allowed preceding the votes (floor nominees are those
       put forward by commissioners against those approved by the Nominating
       Committee)
The committees for which we nominate members:
Administrative Committee (oversees denomination-wide administrative functions
including GA)
Committee on Constitutional Business (gives advice regarding the constitutionality of
overtures from presbyteries to the Assembly; reviews the minutes of the SJC
ensuring that it follows its manual of operation)
Covenant College (reviews actions of the board)
Christian Education and Publications
Covenant Theological Seminary (reviews actions of the board)
Interchurch Relations (oversees the relationship of the PCA to other Presbyterian and
Reformed denominations)
Mission to North America (oversees the work of our denomination’s domestic church
planting agency)
Mission to the World (oversees the work of our denomination’s foreign missionary
agency)
PCA Foundation (oversees our denomination’s agency tasked with encouraging
charitable giving, endowments, etc.)
PCA Retirement and Benefits (oversees retirement investment services)
Ridge Haven (oversees actions of the board)
Reformed University Ministries (oversees our denomination’s campus ministry
agency)
Standing Judicial Commission (functions as the final court of appeal in our
denomination)
Theological Examining Committee (examines all first and second level administration
officers in denominational committees)
1. Additions (157 elders in 55 presbyteries)
2. News
-There is an opening in a good church for an Associate/Executive pastor. If you are interested in talking about
this confidentially please contact me: jkes...@npc-dublin.org and I’ll gladly talk with you about the position.
You may not share this information with anyone outside the NP

-There is also an opening at MTW for a Chief Administrative Officer:
 "Mission to the World, the missions sending agency of the Presbyterian Church in America, is seeking a
leader with a passion for mobilizing the Church to see Christ worshiped by every people, tribe and tongue.
Specifically, we are looking for an experienced person who is gifted to serve the missionaries of our
organization and help us to pursue our mission and values. Amid the complexities inherent to nonprofit
organizations, international mobilization and management, we are looking for a leader who can streamline
processes, achieve efficiency in administration, while remaining responsive to the missionary community.
 The Chief Administrative Officer (CAO) is a Senior Team position that reports to MTW’s Chief Operating
Officer (COO). Through a cadre of experienced directors, the CAO oversees the Accounting, Treasury, Human
Resources, Process Analysis and Training, and Information Technology departments of the home office.
Specifically, this position is responsible for: oversight of cash and investment management; maintaining the
financial and legal policies of the organization; and directing the process and technology resources to help
carry out our strategic decisions. Key in the profile of this position will be a desire to participate in MTW’s
missional community, embrace and proclaim the gospel of grace, and apply the services of this division
responsibly and creatively to fuel transformative church planting and renewal movements.” Please contact
Brian Deringer (brian.d...@mtw.org) with any questions. You may not share this information with anyone
outside the NP

TE Larry Hoop added a discussion to the website discussions page. He has participated in an ad-hoc
committee discussion about how to improve denominational health. As part of that discussion there is a
proposed biennial conference. They would like feedback from the National
Partnership : http://thenationalpartnership.com/national-conference-to-minister-to-ministers/

2. News

Below is a note from David Richter, Church Planter for Christ The King in Somerville, MA (Boston). Please pray for
the Richters as they plant!

Dear Brothers,

James was gracious enough to let me write a quick note to all of you regarding a particular need that my
church plant will be facing in the New Year. My wife Kelly and I moved to Boston almost two years ago now
with our family to plant a church in Somerville, MA (an inner ring city right in the heart of Metro-Boston), with
the Christ the King Church Planting Network. Since that time, the Lord has been very gracious to us. We have
seen a number of conversions. We have seen sleepy Christians in our community awaken to the transforming
power of the gospel of grace. Moreover, we have seen our core group grow to the extent that we were able to
launch weekly public services on September 7th.
I can’t tell you how exciting and thankful we are for all of this! The need here is enormous. As many of you
know, New England is currently the most un-churched region in the country, with only 2.5% of our population
claiming to be evangelical Christian. Therefore, it is a great honor to be given the gift of serving the Lord in
this area of the country.



Yet, as you can imagine, ministry in Boston is not cheap. We were able to initially raise a lot of money to move
onto the field and sustain us for the first couple of years. But just recently we received word from MNA that
unless things change with our support account, we will not have enough income after the first of the year to
continue at our current rate of expenditure. Therefore, I come to you asking for your prayers, brothers. Please
pray for the Lord's continued molding of our hearts, the strengthening of our faith, the increased effectiveness
of our ministry, and for His abundant provision for our needs.



Moreover, if you know of anyone, or of any church, that might be interested and/or willing to support our
church plant, please let me know - davidr...@ctksomerville.org.

Here is the link to our website (www.ctksomerville.org) and our MNA support page
(https://www.egsnetwork.com/gift2/?giftid=44257F0B6C6942A).

Please don’t hesitate to contact me if you have any questions or if you would like to hear more about what the
Lord has been doing here in Boston.

Grace & Peace,

David Richter

staff work, etc. These churches are all healthy and strategic in our denomination. Please feel free to contact me if
you would like me to put you in touch.

Denomination Work: you are needed!

PERMANENT COMMITTEES:
We’ve mentioned the value of participation in the Review of Presbytery Records committee and the
Nominating Committee as two key ways you can do your part to preserve the health of our denomination.
Participation in the NP means, at a minimum, that you will be involved in helping to fill committee spots from
your presbytery. Either volunteer yourself for open slots on RPR and the Nominating Committee (The NC
recommends members for the permanent committees and the SJC to the floor of the General Assembly) or
find a friend in your presbytery to volunteer. Your Winter meeting is the likely point for nominations to these
committees.

GA COMMITTEE OF COMMISSIONERS:
The CoC is the group of presbyters that meet during the first days of General Assembly to review the work of
the permanent committees and, in the case of the Overtures Committee, make recommendations for the
business passed to the assemblies through her presbyteries. The primary way we influence the disposition of
our denomination is through work like this. If you’re a part of the National Partnership we expect that you’re
ready to do your duty so please ask your presbyteries to place you on these committees. In the order of
influence on the work of the assembly: Overtures Committee, Administrative Committee, Interchurch
Relations. Overtures, in particular, acts as a sort of “Senate” for the assembly. Each presbytery sends both a
RE and TE and the work of this committee cannot be amended from the floor of the assembly. We need good
men to serve in these places if the majority in our denomination intends to steer the ship.
If those positions are filled I would encourage you to volunteer anywhere you can.

Please let me know if you are on a CoC, Nominating Committee, or Review of Presbytery Records so we can
keep our rosters current

overtures coming to the assembly.

Brothers,
So far we are counting four NP men on the Overtures committee, which is extremely low. Please check with your
presbytery clerk and try to fill those open spaces. The OC functions as a sort of Senate in our new rules, meaning
that any amending/perfecting of overtures from presbyteries takes place in that meeting. The OC also functions as
a sort of gateway for overtures that should not see the floor of the Assembly. This is a vital way to serve our
denomination. Help us fill spots on the OC, Nom Com, RPR as first priorities.

GA COMMITTEE OF COMMISSIONERS:
The CoC is the group of presbyters that meet during the first days of General Assembly to review the work of the
permanent committees and, in the case of the Overtures Committee, make recommendations for the business
passed to the assemblies through her presbyteries. The primary way we influence the disposition of our
denomination is through work like this. If you’re a part of the National Partnership we expect that you’re ready to
do your duty so please ask your presbyteries to place you on these committees. In the order of influence on the
work of the assembly: Overtures Committee, Administrative Committee, Interchurch Relations. Overtures, in
particular, acts as a sort of “Senate” for the assembly. Each presbytery sends both a RE and TE and the work of this
committee cannot be amended from the floor of the assembly. We need good men to serve in these places if the
majority in our denomination intends to steer the ship.
If those positions are filled I would encourage you to volunteer anywhere you can.

Partnership men on the Overtures Committee
David Richter (Southern New England), Sean Roberts (Northern New England), Geoff Ziegler (Chicago Metro), Bent
Stutzman (Chicago Metro)
Please let me know if you are on a CoC, Nominating Committee, or Review of Presbytery Records so we can keep

Brothers,
So far we are counting 11 NP men on the Overtures committee, which is low. Please check with your presbytery
clerk and try to fill those open spaces. The OC functions as a sort of Senate in our new rules, meaning that any
amending/perfecting of overtures from presbyteries takes place in that meeting. The OC also functions as a sort of
gateway for overtures that should not see the floor of the Assembly. This is a vital way to serve our denomination.
Help us fill spots on the OC and RPR as first priorities.


1. Additions (163 in 58 presbyteries)
TE JonPaul Watson (Eastern Carolina)


2. News
-Overture 7, which requires officers to testify in church court proceedings, was rejected by Potomac Presbytery but
submitted to the General Assembly from the session where it was first crafted (David Coffin’s church, Hope PCA in
Fairfax VA). Historically our polity has provided a protection for the accused to decline testimony in a case. While
some are concerned that continuing to allow this right encourages dishonesty in matters as serious as
constitutional subscription others are concerned that compelling testimony could submit some to repeated calls to
clarify their positions – in effect re-examining a man repeatedly without cause. I’ve opened discussion on this
overture on the site: http://thenationalpartnership.com/overture-7/ and have provided the rationale presented at
Potomac’s meeting to defeat the overture. The rationale in favor of the Overture is of course included in the
language itself.
-We’ve opened discussion on the website (http://thenationalpartnership.com) regarding this year's first three
Overtures of substance: Overture 2, North Texas (study committee on Sabbath) and Overture 3, Gulf Coast
(changes to baptism charge) and Overture 7, Hope PCA Fairfax, VA (compel officer testimony)
-Willow Creek Church (Pastor Kevin Labby, NP member) is looking for a Worship/Music Director. Here’s a link to the
position: http://www.pcaac.org/wp-content/uploads/2015/02/DirofMusic_WillowCreekWinterSpringsFL.pdf

2. News

3. Action items:
Denomination Work: you are needed!


GA COMMITTEE OF COMMISSIONERS:
The CoC is the group of presbyters that meet during the first days of General Assembly to review the work of the
permanent committees and, in the case of the Overtures Committee, make recommendations for the business
passed to the assemblies through her presbyteries. The primary way we influence the disposition of our
denomination is through work like this. If you’re a part of the National Partnership we expect that you’re ready to
do your duty so please ask your presbyteries to place you on these committees. In the order of influence on the
work of the assembly: Overtures Committee, Administrative Committee, Interchurch Relations. Overtures, in
particular, acts as a sort of “Senate” for the assembly. Each presbytery sends both a RE and TE and the work of this
committee cannot be amended from the floor of the assembly. We need good men to serve in these places if the
majority in our denomination intends to steer the ship.
If those positions are filled I would encourage you to volunteer anywhere you can.
